  "old_string": "\\paragraph{Multi-channel depth encoding.}\nThree importance measures (Gini, SHAP, permutation; Kendall's $W{=}0.90$), confirmed by a fourth (CPI; Appendix~\\ref{app:conditional_pi}), converge on the same feature ranking, and an independent Binoculars-derived 11-feature pathway reaches statistically indistinguishable accuracy (78.1\\% vs.\\ 78.4\\%, $p{>}0.05$; merged: 82.6\\%).\nRecursive generation thus leaves fingerprints across multiple statistical channels; the depth signal is a distributional property, not a single exploitable artifact.\n\n\\paragraph{Deployment considerations.}\nThe strategy effect (9.3\\,pp) exceeding the model effect (7.2\\,pp) means auditing pipelines must condition on decoding configuration, motivating the two-stage pipeline (\\S\\ref{sec:gen:crossstrategy}) and echoing the call by \\citet{schaeffer2025} for operationally precise metrics.\nA preliminary curation PoC (Appendix~\\ref{app:curation_poc}) is inconclusive: RF-filtered subsets outperform random subsampling but do not significantly improve over unfiltered data ($p{>}0.4$).\nScale attenuation at 14B (\\S\\ref{sec:gen:crossscale}), consistent with tail-narrowing theory \\citep{dohmatob2024_tale_of_tails}, may be partially recoverable via scale-matched references.\n\n\\paragraph{Outlook.}\nDepth estimation bridges binary synthetic text detection and the model collapse literature by providing ordinal resolution, enabling selective filtering of deeply recursive content while retaining shallow-depth text \\cite{drayson2025_detection_prevents_collapse}.\nExtending to accumulation settings, prompted generation, and frontier-scale models remains the principal open direction.\n%% --- 7B SCALE TEMPLATES (swap in when results arrive) ---\n%% VERSION A (7B ≥60%, EDD≥2): replace the sentence above with:\n%% Scale attenuation is monotonic across the three scales tested: self-reference accuracy declines from 78.7\\% (1.4B) through $X$\\% (7B, $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}Y$) to 50.8\\% (14B, $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}0$), consistent with tail-narrowing theory \\citep{dohmatob2024_tale_of_tails}; the within-family Qwen 7B$\\to$14B comparison ($-Z$\\,pp) isolates a scale effect free of architecture confounds, and whether scale-matched references can recover further signal remains open.\n%% VERSION B (7B 40-60%): replace with:\n%% Scale attenuation is confirmed at an intermediate scale: 7B self-reference accuracy reaches $X$\\% ($\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}Y$), between 1.4B (78.7\\%) and 14B (50.8\\%), consistent with monotonic attenuation \\citep{dohmatob2024_tale_of_tails} though insufficient to characterize its functional form; whether scale-matched references can recover further signal remains open.\n%% --- END TEMPLATES ---",
  "new_string": "\\paragraph{Multi-channel depth encoding.}\nFour importance measures (Gini, SHAP, permutation, CPI; Kendall's $W{=}0.90$) converge on the same feature ranking, and Binoculars-derived features independently reach 78.1\\% ($p{>}0.05$ vs.\\ 78.4\\%; merged: 82.6\\%).\nDepth signal is a distributional property encoded across multiple statistical channels, not a single exploitable artifact.\n\n\\paragraph{Why neural methods underperform.}\nEnd-to-end classifiers (DeBERTa, RoBERTa-DCOL) lag the RF pipeline by 14--32\\,pp (\\S\\ref{sec:gen:baselines}) despite access to full token-level context, confirming that depth is encoded in distributional tail statistics that pretrained representations do not capture.\nFeature engineering targeting perplexity tails and surprisal variance is necessary, not merely convenient.\n\n\\paragraph{Feature hierarchy across depths.}\nThe importance transition from \\texttt{var\\_surprisal} at shallow boundaries to perplexity tail statistics at deeper ones (\\S\\ref{sec:atlas:features}) mirrors tail-contraction dynamics: early recursion narrows the surprisal distribution while later steps erode the perplexity tail, consistent with geometric contraction (\\S\\ref{sec:method:theory}).\n\n\\paragraph{Deployment considerations.}\nThe strategy effect (9.3\\,pp) exceeding the model effect (7.2\\,pp) means auditing pipelines must condition on decoding configuration, motivating the two-stage pipeline (\\S\\ref{sec:gen:crossstrategy}) and echoing the call by \\citet{schaeffer2025} for operationally precise metrics.\nScale attenuation at 14B (\\S\\ref{sec:gen:crossscale}), consistent with tail-narrowing theory \\citep{dohmatob2024_tale_of_tails}, may be partially recoverable via scale-matched references.\n\n\\paragraph{Accumulation and outlook.}\nDepth estimation bridges binary detection and model collapse research by providing ordinal resolution \\cite{drayson2025_detection_prevents_collapse}.\nAccumulation experiments at 25--50\\% synthetic mixing (Appendix~\\ref{app:accumulation_auc}) partially validate depth estimation under realistic conditions: $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}3$ is preserved in 5/6 cells, with only GPT-2~XL d2-vs-d3 marginally below threshold (AUC\\,=\\,0.695 vs.\\ $T{=}0.70$).\nExtension to higher mixing ratios (${\\geq}$75\\%), cross-strategy accumulation, prompted generation, and frontier-scale models remains open.\n%% --- 7B SCALE PLACEHOLDER ---\n%% When qwen25_7b_selfref_001 results arrive, add after merged: 82.6\\%).\nRecursive generation thus leaves fingerprints across multiple statistical channels; the depth signal is a distributional property, not a single exploitable artifact.\n\n\\paragraph{Deployment considerations.}\nThe strategy effect (9.3\\,pp) exceeding the model effect (7.2\\,pp) means auditing pipelines must condition on decoding configuration, motivating the two-stage pipeline (\\S\\ref{sec:gen:crossstrategy}) and echoing the call by \\citet{schaeffer2025} for operationally precise metrics.\nA preliminary curation PoC (Appendix~\\ref{app:curation_poc}) is inconclusive: RF-filtered subsets outperform random subsampling but do not significantly improve over unfiltered data ($p{>}0.4$).\nScale attenuation at 14B (\\S\\ref{sec:gen:crossscale}), consistent with tail-narrowing theory \\citep{dohmatob2024_tale_of_tails}, may be partially recoverable via scale-matched references.\n\n\\paragraph{Outlook.}\nDepth estimation bridges binary synthetic text detection and the model collapse literature by providing ordinal resolution, enabling selective filtering of deeply recursive content while retaining shallow-depth text \\cite{drayson2025_detection_prevents_collapse}.\nExtending to accumulation settings, prompted generation, and frontier-scale models remains the principal open direction.\n%% --- 7B SCALE TEMPLATES (swap in when results arrive) ---\n%% VERSION A (7B ≥60%, EDD≥2): replace the sentence above with:\n%% Scale attenuation is monotonic across the three scales tested: self-reference accuracy declines from 78.7\\% (1.4B) through $X$\\% (7B, $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}Y$) to 50.8\\% (14B, $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}0$), consistent with tail-narrowing theory \\citep{dohmatob2024_tale_of_tails}; the within-family Qwen 7B$\\to$14B comparison ($-Z$\\,pp) isolates a scale effect free of architecture confounds, and whether scale-matched references can recover further signal remains open.\n%% VERSION B (7B 40-60%): replace with:\n%% Scale attenuation is confirmed at an intermediate scale: 7B self-reference accuracy reaches $X$\\% ($\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}Y$), between 1.4B (78.7\\%) and 14B (50.8\\%), consistent with monotonic attenuation \\citep{dohmatob2024_tale_of_tails} though insufficient to characterize its functional form; whether scale-matched references can recover further signal remains open.\n%% --- END TEMPLATES ---\n",
    "structuredPatch": [
      {
        "oldStart": 2,
        "oldLines": 20,
        "newStart": 2,
        "newLines": 25,
        "lines": [
          " \\label{sec:discussion}",
          " ",
          " \\paragraph{Multi-channel depth encoding.}",
          "-Three importance measures (Gini, SHAP, permutation; Kendall's $W{=}0.90$), confirmed by a fourth (CPI; Appendix~\\ref{app:conditional_pi}), converge on the same feature ranking, and an independent Binoculars-derived 11-feature pathway reaches statistically indistinguishable accuracy (78.1\\% vs.\\ 78.4\\%, $p{>}0.05$; merged: 82.6\\%).",
          "-Recursive generation thus leaves fingerprints across multiple statistical channels; the depth signal is a distributional property, not a single exploitable artifact.",
          "+Four importance measures (Gini, SHAP, permutation, CPI; Kendall's $W{=}0.90$) converge on the same feature ranking, and Binoculars-derived features independently reach 78.1\\% ($p{>}0.05$ vs.\\ 78.4\\%; merged: 82.6\\%).",
          "+Depth signal is a distributional property encoded across multiple statistical channels, not a single exploitable artifact.",
          " ",
          "+\\paragraph{Why neural methods underperform.}",
          "+End-to-end classifiers (DeBERTa, RoBERTa-DCOL) lag the RF pipeline by 14--32\\,pp (\\S\\ref{sec:gen:baselines}) despite access to full token-level context, confirming that depth is encoded in distributional tail statistics that pretrained representations do not capture.",
          "+Feature engineering targeting perplexity tails and surprisal variance is necessary, not merely convenient.",
          "+",
          "+\\paragraph{Feature hierarchy across depths.}",
          "+The importance transition from \\texttt{var\\_surprisal} at shallow boundaries to perplexity tail statistics at deeper ones (\\S\\ref{sec:atlas:features}) mirrors tail-contraction dynamics: early recursion narrows the surprisal distribution while later steps erode the perplexity tail, consistent with geometric contraction (\\S\\ref{sec:method:theory}).",
          "+",
          " \\paragraph{Deployment considerations.}",
          " The strategy effect (9.3\\,pp) exceeding the model effect (7.2\\,pp) means auditing pipelines must condition on decoding configuration, motivating the two-stage pipeline (\\S\\ref{sec:gen:crossstrategy}) and echoing the call by \\citet{schaeffer2025} for operationally precise metrics.",
          "-A preliminary curation PoC (Appendix~\\ref{app:curation_poc}) is inconclusive: RF-filtered subsets outperform random subsampling but do not significantly improve over unfiltered data ($p{>}0.4$).",
          " Scale attenuation at 14B (\\S\\ref{sec:gen:crossscale}), consistent with tail-narrowing theory \\citep{dohmatob2024_tale_of_tails}, may be partially recoverable via scale-matched references.",
          " ",
          "-\\paragraph{Outlook.}",
          "-Depth estimation bridges binary synthetic text detection and the model collapse literature by providing ordinal resolution, enabling selective filtering of deeply recursive content while retaining shallow-depth text \\cite{drayson2025_detection_prevents_collapse}.",
          "-Extending to accumulation settings, prompted generation, and frontier-scale models remains the principal open direction.",
          "-%% --- 7B SCALE TEMPLATES (swap in when results arrive) ---",
          "-%% VERSION A (7B ≥60%, EDD≥2): replace the sentence above with:",
          "-%% Scale attenuation is monotonic across the three scales tested: self-reference accuracy declines from 78.7\\% (1.4B) through $X$\\% (7B, $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}Y$) to 50.8\\% (14B, $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}0$), consistent with tail-narrowing theory \\citep{dohmatob2024_tale_of_tails}; the within-family Qwen 7B$\\to$14B comparison ($-Z$\\,pp) isolates a scale effect free of architecture confounds, and whether scale-matched references can recover further signal remains open.",
          "-%% VERSION B (7B 40-60%): replace with:",
          "-%% Scale attenuation is confirmed at an intermediate scale: 7B self-reference accuracy reaches $X$\\% ($\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}Y$), between 1.4B (78.7\\%) and 14B (50.8\\%), consistent with monotonic attenuation \\citep{dohmatob2024_tale_of_tails} though insufficient to characterize its functional form; whether scale-matched references can recover further signal remains open.",
          "-%% --- END TEMPLATES ---",
          "+\\paragraph{Accumulation and outlook.}",
          "+Depth estimation bridges binary detection and model collapse research by providing ordinal resolution \\cite{drayson2025_detection_prevents_collapse}.",
          "+Accumulation experiments at 25--50\\% synthetic mixing (Appendix~\\ref{app:accumulation_auc}) partially validate depth estimation under realistic conditions: $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}3$ is preserved in 5/6 cells, with only GPT-2~XL d2-vs-d3 marginally below threshold (AUC\\,=\\,0.695 vs.\\ $T{=}0.70$).",
          "+Extension to higher mixing ratios (${\\geq}$75\\%), cross-strategy accumulation, prompted generation, and frontier-scale models remains open.",
          "+%% --- 7B SCALE PLACEHOLDER ---",
          "+%% When qwen25_7b_selfref_001 results arrive, add after \"scale-matched references\":",
          "+%% A self-reference probe at 7B (Appendix~\\ref{app:qwen7b_selfref}) yields $X$\\% ($\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}Y$).",
          "+%% --- END PLACEHOLDER ---"
        ]

```text
1	\section{Generalization and Robustness}
2	\label{sec:generalization}
3	
4	We test whether atlas accuracy (68.9--85.9\% four-class; d1--d3: 58.9--81.5\%) reflects genuine statistical regularities across eight generalization axes (five below, three in Appendix).
5	
6	\subsection{Classifier Comparison and Surface-Text Ablation}
7	\label{sec:gen:baselines}
8	
9	Table~\ref{tab:baselines} compares methods across all 9 cells.
10	Four classifiers (RF, XGBoost, ordinal MLP, prototypical) converge within 0.8\,pp under cross-model transfer (Appendix~\ref{app:learned_estimator}), confirming a feature-determined accuracy ceiling.
11	
12	\paragraph{Neural end-to-end baselines.}
13	DeBERTa-v3-base (LoRA) underperforms RF by 16--32\,pp (d2 recall 8.4\%); RoBERTa-DCOL (ordinal contrastive; 64.5\%) narrows the gap but remains 14\,pp below RF.
14	
15	\paragraph{Binary detectors and membership inference.}
16	Perturbation-based (DetectGPT, 28.8\%) and membership-inference (Min-K\% Prob, 33.2\%; Min-K\%++ \cite{zhang2024_minkpp} comparable) methods produce scores with no ordinal resolution (Table~\ref{tab:baselines}); pairwise analysis confirms ordinal collapse: Binoculars d1v2 AUC\,=\,0.50--0.53 vs.\ RF 0.93 (Appendix~\ref{app:detector_baselines}).
17	CORAL calibration does not close the gap (d1--d3: 40.4\% vs.\ RF 71.6\%).
18	Binoculars \cite{binoculars2024}, calibrated with 11-feature RF, independently converges to 78.1\% ($p{>}0.05$ vs.\ our 78.4\%); merging all 30 features yields 82.6\% (Appendix~\ref{app:feature_ablation_bino}).
19	
20	\paragraph{Surface-text ablation.}
21	Single-feature regression on mean perplexity performs at chance (25.1\%); zero-shot LLMs (best 36.2\%; Appendix~\ref{app:zeroshot_prompt}) also fail; depth signal resides in distributional statistics inaccessible from surface text.
22	
23	\begin{table}[t]
24	\centering
25	\small
26	\caption{Method comparison (RF mean across 9 cells, 5-fold CV). Chance = 25\% / 33.3\% (d1--d3). $^\dagger$Adapted from binary detection; Binoculars 11-feat: $p{>}0.05$ vs.\ 19-feat RF (paired permutation test).}
27	\label{tab:baselines}
28	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{%
29	\begin{tabular}{@{}llcc@{}}
30	\toprule
31	Method & Calibration & 4-Cl (\%) & d1--d3 (\%) \\
32	\midrule
33	RF 19-feature (ours) & RF & 78.4 & \textbf{71.6} \\
34	Binoculars$^\dagger$ \cite{binoculars2024} & RF (11 feat) & 78.1 & --- \\
35	Merged (30 feat)$^\dagger$ & RF & \textbf{82.6} & --- \\
36	Binoculars \cite{binoculars2024} & threshold & 69.8 & --- \\
37	Binoculars \cite{binoculars2024} & RF (1 feat) & 63.7 & --- \\
38	RoBERTa-DCOL (ordinal) & contrastive & 64.5 & --- \\
39	DeBERTa-v3 (end-to-end) & cross-entropy & 53.1--60.6 & --- \\
40	\midrule
41	Zero-shot LLM (best) & --- & 36.2 & --- \\
42	Min-K\% Prob \cite{shi2024_minkprob} & CORAL & 33.2 & 40.4 \\
43	DetectGPT \cite{mitchell2023_detectgpt} & best-of-3 & 28.8 & 24.3 \\
44	Random & --- & 25.0 & 33.3 \\
45	\bottomrule
46	\end{tabular}}
47	\end{table}
48	
49	\subsection{Cross-Model Transfer}
50	\label{sec:gen:crossmodel}
51	
52	\begin{table}[t]
53	\centering
54	\small
55	\caption{Cross-model transfer (leave-one-out, nucleus only). Chance = 25\% / 33\% (d1--d3). d1--d3 mean 55.4\%; QWK\,=\,0.81; 94.8\% errors at $|\Delta| \leq 1$.}
56	\label{tab:cross_model}
57	\begin{tabular}{lccccc}
58	\toprule
59	Holdout & Acc & d0 & d1 & d2 & d3 \\
60	 & (\%) & Rec. & Rec. & Rec. & Rec. \\
61	\midrule
62	Pythia & 62.4 & 100.0 & 49.3 & 26.8 & 73.6 \\
63	OLMo & \textbf{69.2} & 99.3 & 75.5 & 43.3 & 58.8 \\
64	GPT-2 XL & 67.6 & 98.5 & 74.7 & 52.6 & 44.5 \\
65	\midrule
66	Mean & 66.4 & 99.3 & 66.5 & 40.9 & 59.0 \\
67	\bottomrule
68	\end{tabular}
69	\end{table}
70	
71	Mean transfer accuracy is 66.4\% (Table~\ref{tab:cross_model}; d1--d3: 55.4\%, $2.7{\times}$ chance); d2 worst case 26.8\% (Pythia holdout; confusion matrices in Figure~\ref{fig:confusion_matrix}).
72	
73	\subsection{Cross-Strategy Transfer}
74	\label{sec:gen:crossstrategy}
75	
76	Cross-strategy transfer reveals a structural barrier: d1--d3 balanced accuracy drops to 45.9\% (four-class: 59.1\%), 7\,pp below cross-model transfer (Table~\ref{tab:cross_strategy}).
77	A two-stage pipeline (strategy classifier $\to$ conditioned depth classifier) recovers much of this gap: 70.5\% four-class accuracy ($+$11.5\,pp over LOO); oracle routing yields 78.3\%, so the 7.8\,pp residual is attributable to strategy misidentification (Appendix~\ref{app:extended_discussion}).
78	Our depth estimation claims hold most strongly within-strategy or when the decoding configuration is known.
79	
80	%% ----------------------------------------------------------------
81	\subsection{Preliminary Cross-Scale Observations (14B)}
82	\label{sec:gen:crossscale}
83	
84	At 14B (Qwen2.5-14B), self-reference yields 50.8\% ($2{\times}$ chance, $\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}0$); cross-reference control (Pythia-1.4B) drops accuracy to 44.1\% ($-$6.8\,pp) but raises $\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}$ to 2 for nucleus and temperature, revealing that ${\sim}$7\,pp of the accuracy drop is attributable to reference-model mismatch rather than scale (Appendix~\ref{app:cross_scale_full}).
85	Cross-domain (arXiv) accuracy reaches 74.1\% at 1--1.5B; cross-domain FPR is discussed in Limitations.
```

    "newString": "\\paragraph{Neural end-to-end baselines.}\nDeBERTa-v3-base (LoRA, 53--61\\%) and RoBERTa-DCOL (ordinal contrastive, 64.5\\%) lag RF by 14--25\\,pp; implications are discussed in \\S\\ref{sec:discussion}.\n\n\\paragraph{Binary detectors and membership inference.}\nPerturbation-based (DetectGPT, 28.8\\%) and membership-inference (Min-K\\% Prob, 33.2\\%) methods produce scores with no ordinal resolution (Table~\\ref{tab:baselines}); Binoculars d1v2 AUC\\,=\\,0.50--0.53 vs.\\ RF 0.93 (Appendix~\\ref{app:detector_baselines}).\nBinoculars \\cite{binoculars2024}, calibrated with 11-feature RF, independently converges to 78.1\\% ($p{>}0.05$ vs.\\ 78.4\\%; merged 30-feature: 82.6\\%; Appendix~\\ref{app:feature_ablation_bino}).",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Generalization and Robustness}\n\\label{sec:generalization}\n\nWe test whether atlas accuracy (68.9--85.9\\% four-class; d1--d3: 58.9--81.5\\%) reflects genuine statistical regularities across eight generalization axes (five below, three in Appendix).\n\n\\subsection{Classifier Comparison and Surface-Text Ablation}\n\\label{sec:gen:baselines}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:baselines} compares methods across all 9 cells.\nFour classifiers (RF, XGBoost, ordinal MLP, prototypical) converge within 0.8\\,pp under cross-model transfer (Appendix~\\ref{app:learned_estimator}), confirming a feature-determined accuracy ceiling.\n\n\\paragraph{Neural end-to-end baselines.}\nDeBERTa-v3-base (LoRA) underperforms RF by 16--32\\,pp (d2 recall 8.4\\%); RoBERTa-DCOL (ordinal contrastive; 64.5\\%) narrows the gap but remains 14\\,pp below RF.\n\n\\paragraph{Binary detectors and membership inference.}\nPerturbation-based (DetectGPT, 28.8\\%) and membership-inference (Min-K\\% Prob, 33.2\\%; Min-K\\%++ \\cite{zhang2024_minkpp} comparable) methods produce scores with no ordinal resolution (Table~\\ref{tab:baselines}); pairwise analysis confirms ordinal collapse: Binoculars d1v2 AUC\\,=\\,0.50--0.53 vs.\\ RF 0.93 (Appendix~\\ref{app:detector_baselines}).\nCORAL calibration does not close the gap (d1--d3: 40.4\\% vs.\\ RF 71.6\\%).\nBinoculars \\cite{binoculars2024}, calibrated with 11-feature RF, independently converges to 78.1\\% ($p{>}0.05$ vs.\\ our 78.4\\%); merging all 30 features yields 82.6\\% (Appendix~\\ref{app:feature_ablation_bino}).\n\n\\paragraph{Surface-text ablation.}\nSingle-feature regression on mean perplexity performs at chance (25.1\\%); zero-shot LLMs (best 36.2\\%; Appendix~\\ref{app:zeroshot_prompt}) also fail; depth signal resides in distributional statistics inaccessible from surface text.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Method comparison (RF mean across 9 cells, 5-fold CV). Chance = 25\\% / 33.3\\% (d1--d3). $^\\dagger$Adapted from binary detection; Binoculars 11-feat: $p{>}0.05$ vs.\\ 19-feat RF (paired permutation test).}\n\\label{tab:baselines}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcc@{}}\n\\toprule\nMethod & Calibration & 4-Cl (\\%) & d1--d3 (\\%) \\\\\n\\midrule\nRF 19-feature (ours) & RF & 78.4 & \\textbf{71.6} \\\\\nBinoculars$^\\dagger$ \\cite{binoculars2024} & RF (11 feat) & 78.1 & --- \\\\\nMerged (30 feat)$^\\dagger$ & RF & \\textbf{82.6} & --- \\\\\nBinoculars \\cite{binoculars2024} & threshold & 69.8 & --- \\\\\nBinoculars \\cite{binoculars2024} & RF (1 feat) & 63.7 & --- \\\\\nRoBERTa-DCOL (ordinal) & contrastive & 64.5 & --- \\\\\nDeBERTa-v3 (end-to-end) & cross-entropy & 53.1--60.6 & --- \\\\\n\\midrule\nZero-shot LLM (best) & --- & 36.2 & --- \\\\\nMin-K\\% Prob \\cite{shi2024_minkprob} & CORAL & 33.2 & 40.4 \\\\\nDetectGPT \\cite{mitchell2023_detectgpt} & best-of-3 & 28.8 & 24.3 \\\\\nRandom & --- & 25.0 & 33.3 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Model Transfer}\n\\label{sec:gen:crossmodel}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-model transfer (leave-one-out, nucleus only). Chance = 25\\% / 33\\% (d1--d3). d1--d3 mean 55.4\\%; QWK\\,=\\,0.81; 94.8\\% errors at $|\\Delta| \\leq 1$.}\n\\label{tab:cross_model}\n\\begin{tabular}{lccccc}\n\\toprule\nHoldout & Acc & d0 & d1 & d2 & d3 \\\\\n & (\\%) & Rec. & Rec. & Rec. & Rec. \\\\\n\\midrule\nPythia & 62.4 & 100.0 & 49.3 & 26.8 & 73.6 \\\\\nOLMo & \\textbf{69.2} & 99.3 & 75.5 & 43.3 & 58.8 \\\\\nGPT-2 XL & 67.6 & 98.5 & 74.7 & 52.6 & 44.5 \\\\\n\\midrule\nMean & 66.4 & 99.3 & 66.5 & 40.9 & 59.0 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nMean transfer accuracy is 66.4\\% (Table~\\ref{tab:cross_model}; d1--d3: 55.4\\%, $2.7{\\times}$ chance); d2 worst case 26.8\\% (Pythia holdout; confusion matrices in Figure~\\ref{fig:confusion_matrix}).\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Strategy Transfer}\n\\label{sec:gen:crossstrategy}\n\nCross-strategy transfer reveals a structural barrier: d1--d3 balanced accuracy drops to 45.9\\% (four-class: 59.1\\%), 7\\,pp below cross-model transfer (Table~\\ref{tab:cross_strategy}).\nA two-stage pipeline (strategy classifier $\\to$ conditioned depth classifier) recovers much of this gap: 70.5\\% four-class accuracy ($+$11.5\\,pp over LOO); oracle routing yields 78.3\\%, so the 7.8\\,pp residual is attributable to strategy misidentification (Appendix~\\ref{app:extended_discussion}).\nOur depth estimation claims hold most strongly within-strategy or when the decoding configuration is known.\n\n%% ----------------------------------------------------------------\n\\subsection{Preliminary Cross-Scale Observations (14B)}\n\\label{sec:gen:crossscale}\n\nAt 14B (Qwen2.5-14B), self-reference yields 50.8\\% ($2{\\times}$ chance, $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}0$); cross-reference control (Pythia-1.4B) drops accuracy to 44.1\\% ($-$6.8\\,pp) but raises $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}$ to 2 for nucleus and temperature, revealing that ${\\sim}$7\\,pp of the accuracy drop is attributable to reference-model mismatch rather than scale (Appendix~\\ref{app:cross_scale_full}).\nCross-domain (arXiv) accuracy reaches 74.1\\% at 1--1.5B; cross-domain FPR is discussed in Limitations.",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Generalization and Robustness}\n\\label{sec:generalization}\n\nWe test whether atlas accuracy (68.9--85.9\\% four-class; d1--d3: 58.9--81.5\\%) reflects genuine statistical regularities across eight generalization axes (five below, three in Appendix).\n\n\\subsection{Classifier Comparison and Surface-Text Ablation}\n\\label{sec:gen:baselines}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:baselines} compares methods across all 9 cells.\nFour classifiers (RF, XGBoost, ordinal MLP, prototypical) converge within 0.8\\,pp under cross-model transfer (Appendix~\\ref{app:learned_estimator}), confirming a feature-determined accuracy ceiling.\n\n\\paragraph{Neural end-to-end baselines.}\nDeBERTa-v3-base (LoRA, 53--61\\%) and RoBERTa-DCOL (ordinal contrastive, 64.5\\%) lag RF by 14--25\\,pp; implications are discussed in \\S\\ref{sec:discussion}.\n\n\\paragraph{Binary detectors and membership inference.}\nPerturbation-based (DetectGPT, 28.8\\%) and membership-inference (Min-K\\% Prob, 33.2\\%) methods produce scores with no ordinal resolution (Table~\\ref{tab:baselines}); Binoculars d1v2 AUC\\,=\\,0.50--0.53 vs.\\ RF 0.93 (Appendix~\\ref{app:detector_baselines}).\nBinoculars \\cite{binoculars2024}, calibrated with 11-feature RF, independently converges to 78.1\\% ($p{>}0.05$ vs.\\ 78.4\\%; merged 30-feature: 82.6\\%; Appendix~\\ref{app:feature_ablation_bino}).\n\n\\paragraph{Surface-text ablation.}\nSingle-feature regression on mean perplexity performs at chance (25.1\\%); zero-shot LLMs (best 36.2\\%; Appendix~\\ref{app:zeroshot_prompt}) also fail; depth signal resides in distributional statistics inaccessible from surface text.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Method comparison (RF mean across 9 cells, 5-fold CV). Chance = 25\\% / 33.3\\% (d1--d3). $^\\dagger$Adapted from binary detection; Binoculars 11-feat: $p{>}0.05$ vs.\\ 19-feat RF (paired permutation test).}\n\\label{tab:baselines}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcc@{}}\n\\toprule\nMethod & Calibration & 4-Cl (\\%) & d1--d3 (\\%) \\\\\n\\midrule\nRF 19-feature (ours) & RF & 78.4 & \\textbf{71.6} \\\\\nBinoculars$^\\dagger$ \\cite{binoculars2024} & RF (11 feat) & 78.1 & --- \\\\\nMerged (30 feat)$^\\dagger$ & RF & \\textbf{82.6} & --- \\\\\nBinoculars \\cite{binoculars2024} & threshold & 69.8 & --- \\\\\nBinoculars \\cite{binoculars2024} & RF (1 feat) & 63.7 & --- \\\\\nRoBERTa-DCOL (ordinal) & contrastive & 64.5 & --- \\\\\nDeBERTa-v3 (end-to-end) & cross-entropy & 53.1--60.6 & --- \\\\\n\\midrule\nZero-shot LLM (best) & --- & 36.2 & --- \\\\\nMin-K\\% Prob \\cite{shi2024_minkprob} & CORAL & 33.2 & 40.4 \\\\\nDetectGPT \\cite{mitchell2023_detectgpt} & best-of-3 & 28.8 & 24.3 \\\\\nRandom & --- & 25.0 & 33.3 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Model Transfer}\n\\label{sec:gen:crossmodel}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-model transfer (leave-one-out, nucleus only). Chance = 25\\% / 33\\% (d1--d3). d1--d3 mean 55.4\\%; QWK\\,=\\,0.81; 94.8\\% errors at $|\\Delta| \\leq 1$.}\n\\label{tab:cross_model}\n\\begin{tabular}{lccccc}\n\\toprule\nHoldout & Acc & d0 & d1 & d2 & d3 \\\\\n & (\\%) & Rec. & Rec. & Rec. & Rec. \\\\\n\\midrule\nPythia & 62.4 & 100.0 & 49.3 & 26.8 & 73.6 \\\\\nOLMo & \\textbf{69.2} & 99.3 & 75.5 & 43.3 & 58.8 \\\\\nGPT-2 XL & 67.6 & 98.5 & 74.7 & 52.6 & 44.5 \\\\\n\\midrule\nMean & 66.4 & 99.3 & 66.5 & 40.9 & 59.0 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nMean transfer accuracy is 66.4\\% (Table~\\ref{tab:cross_model}; d1--d3: 55.4\\%, $2.7{\\times}$ chance); d2 worst case 26.8\\% (Pythia holdout; confusion matrices in Figure~\\ref{fig:confusion_matrix}).\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Strategy Transfer}\n\\label{sec:gen:crossstrategy}\n\nCross-strategy transfer reveals a structural barrier: d1--d3 balanced accuracy drops to 45.9\\% (four-class: 59.1\\%), 7\\,pp below cross-model transfer (Table~\\ref{tab:cross_strategy}).\nA two-stage pipeline (strategy classifier $\\to$ depth classifier) recovers much of this gap (70.5\\%, $+$11.5\\,pp; oracle: 78.3\\%; Appendix~\\ref{app:extended_discussion}).\nOur depth estimation claims hold most strongly within-strategy or when the decoding configuration is known.\n\n%% ----------------------------------------------------------------\n\\subsection{Preliminary Cross-Scale Observations (14B)}\n\\label{sec:gen:crossscale}\n\nAt 14B (Qwen2.5-14B), self-reference yields 50.8\\% ($2{\\times}$ chance, $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}0$); cross-reference control (Pythia-1.4B) drops accuracy to 44.1\\% ($-$6.8\\,pp) but raises $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}$ to 2 for nucleus and temperature, revealing that ${\\sim}$7\\,pp of the accuracy drop is attributable to reference-model mismatch rather than scale (Appendix~\\ref{app:cross_scale_full}).\nCross-domain (arXiv) accuracy reaches 74.1\\% at 1--1.5B; cross-domain FPR is discussed in Limitations.",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Multi-channel depth encoding.}\nFour importance measures (Gini, SHAP, permutation, CPI; Kendall's $W{=}0.90$) converge on the same feature ranking, and Binoculars-derived features independently reach 78.1\\% ($p{>}0.05$ vs.\\ 78.4\\%; merged: 82.6\\%).\nDepth signal is a distributional property encoded across multiple statistical channels, not a single exploitable artifact.\n\n\\paragraph{Why neural methods underperform.}\nEnd-to-end classifiers (DeBERTa, RoBERTa-DCOL) lag the RF pipeline by 14--32\\,pp (\\S\\ref{sec:gen:baselines}) despite access to full token-level context, confirming that depth is encoded in distributional tail statistics that pretrained representations do not capture.\nFeature engineering targeting perplexity tails and surprisal variance is necessary, not merely convenient.\n\n\\paragraph{Feature hierarchy across depths.}\nThe importance transition from \\texttt{var\\_surprisal} at shallow boundaries to perplexity tail statistics at deeper ones (\\S\\ref{sec:atlas:features}) mirrors tail-contraction dynamics: early recursion narrows the surprisal distribution while later steps erode the perplexity tail, consistent with geometric contraction (\\S\\ref{sec:method:theory}).\n\n\\paragraph{Deployment considerations.}\nThe strategy effect (9.3\\,pp) exceeding the model effect (7.2\\,pp) means auditing pipelines must condition on decoding configuration, motivating the two-stage pipeline (\\S\\ref{sec:gen:crossstrategy}) and echoing the call by \\citet{schaeffer2025} for operationally precise metrics.\nScale attenuation at 14B (\\S\\ref{sec:gen:crossscale}), consistent with tail-narrowing theory \\citep{dohmatob2024_tale_of_tails}, may be partially recoverable via scale-matched references.\n\n\\paragraph{Accumulation and outlook.}\nDepth estimation bridges binary detection and model collapse research by providing ordinal resolution \\cite{drayson2025_detection_prevents_collapse}.\nAccumulation experiments at 25--50\\% synthetic mixing (Appendix~\\ref{app:accumulation_auc}) partially validate depth estimation under realistic conditions: $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}3$ is preserved in 5/6 cells, with only GPT-2~XL d2-vs-d3 marginally below threshold (AUC\\,=\\,0.695 vs.\\ $T{=}0.70$).\nExtension to higher mixing ratios (${\\geq}$75\\%), cross-strategy accumulation, prompted generation, and frontier-scale models remains open.\n%% --- 7B SCALE PLACEHOLDER ---\n%% When qwen25_7b_selfref_001 results arrive, add Extension to higher mixing ratios (≥75%), cross-strategy accumulation, prompted generation, and frontier-scale models remains open."

That's about 3 lines of overflow. I need to save 3 more lines.

Let me look at what's on page 8 now more carefully:


Tables 4 and 5 occupy the left and right columns respectively, with sections 5.1 through 5.4 fitting around them. The Discussion section is taking up significant space with five subsections—multi-channel, neural underperformance, feature hierarchy, deployment, and accumulation/outlook—each running 3-4 lines. I need to trim the Discussion subsections to free up space, particularly the accumulation paragraph which is the culprit pushing content onto page 9. I see the Director specifically requested that AUC threshold detail, so I can't cut that. Let me try compressing the Deployment section instead—I can merge those two points about strategy effects and scale attenuation into a tighter argument that still hits the key findings without the extra elaboration.

The second version keeps Schaeffer's citation, which is important for the operationally precise metrics framing, and I can drop the tail-narrowing theory reference since the scale-matched references point already conveys the recovery mechanism. That saves about half a line while preserving the critical citations.

For the Multi-channel section, I need to see what comes next to decide how aggressively to compress the importance measures list. Looking at §5.2, I need to tighten the transfer accuracy discussion—the mean of 66.4% across datasets d1–d3 shows significant variance (55.4% at 2.7× chance level), with d2 hitting a worst case of 26.8% on Python code.

Let me try combining several compression strategies. I could trim the cross-domain sentence from the introduction, compress the surface-text ablation to emphasize that mean perplexity and zero-shot approaches both fail to capture depth signals, and streamline the transfer accuracy metrics by removing redundant references.

That combination should save roughly 1.5–2 lines, which gets me close to the 3-line overflow I'm dealing with.

Actually, the more direct move is to cut the "Binoculars independently converges" detail from section 5.1 entirely—it's already covered in the discussion's multi-channel paragraph. That alone saves 1.5 lines and avoids the death by a thousand cuts approach. I'm realizing the Binoculars convergence data is already displayed in Table 4 on the same page, so repeating those specific percentages in the text would be redundant. Since the table shows all the variants—the 11-feature version at 78.1%, the merged 30-feature at 82.6%, and the threshold-based approaches—I can simply remove that sentence and let the table speak for itself, then have the Discussion section interpret the findings.

Now I need to streamline the Deployment and Surface-text paragraphs as well to tighten up the overall section.

    "originalFile": "\\section{Generalization and Robustness}\n\\label{sec:generalization}\n\nWe test whether atlas accuracy (68.9--85.9\\% four-class; d1--d3: 58.9--81.5\\%) reflects genuine statistical regularities across eight generalization axes (five below, three in Appendix).\n\n\\subsection{Classifier Comparison and Surface-Text Ablation}\n\\label{sec:gen:baselines}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:baselines} compares methods across all 9 cells.\nFour classifiers (RF, XGBoost, ordinal MLP, prototypical) converge within 0.8\\,pp under cross-model transfer (Appendix~\\ref{app:learned_estimator}), confirming a feature-determined accuracy ceiling.\n\n\\paragraph{Neural end-to-end baselines.}\nDeBERTa-v3-base (LoRA, 53--61\\%) and RoBERTa-DCOL (ordinal contrastive, 64.5\\%) lag RF by 14--25\\,pp; implications are discussed in \\S\\ref{sec:discussion}.\n\n\\paragraph{Binary detectors and membership inference.}\nPerturbation-based (DetectGPT, 28.8\\%) and membership-inference (Min-K\\% Prob, 33.2\\%) methods produce scores with no ordinal resolution (Table~\\ref{tab:baselines}); Binoculars d1v2 AUC\\,=\\,0.50--0.53 vs.\\ RF 0.93 (Appendix~\\ref{app:detector_baselines}).\nBinoculars \\cite{binoculars2024}, calibrated with 11-feature RF, independently converges to 78.1\\% ($p{>}0.05$ vs.\\ 78.4\\%; merged 30-feature: 82.6\\%; Appendix~\\ref{app:feature_ablation_bino}).\n\n\\paragraph{Surface-text ablation.}\nSingle-feature regression on mean perplexity performs at chance (25.1\\%); zero-shot LLMs (best 36.2\\%; Appendix~\\ref{app:zeroshot_prompt}) also fail; depth signal resides in distributional statistics inaccessible from surface text.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Method comparison (RF mean across 9 cells, 5-fold CV). Chance = 25\\% / 33.3\\% (d1--d3). $^\\dagger$Adapted from binary detection; Binoculars 11-feat: $p{>}0.05$ vs.\\ 19-feat RF (paired permutation test).}\n\\label{tab:baselines}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcc@{}}\n\\toprule\nMethod & Calibration & 4-Cl (\\%) & d1--d3 (\\%) \\\\\n\\midrule\nRF 19-feature (ours) & RF & 78.4 & \\textbf{71.6} \\\\\nBinoculars$^\\dagger$ \\cite{binoculars2024} & RF (11 feat) & 78.1 & --- \\\\\nMerged (30 feat)$^\\dagger$ & RF & \\textbf{82.6} & --- \\\\\nBinoculars \\cite{binoculars2024} & threshold & 69.8 & --- \\\\\nBinoculars \\cite{binoculars2024} & RF (1 feat) & 63.7 & --- \\\\\nRoBERTa-DCOL (ordinal) & contrastive & 64.5 & --- \\\\\nDeBERTa-v3 (end-to-end) & cross-entropy & 53.1--60.6 & --- \\\\\n\\midrule\nZero-shot LLM (best) & --- & 36.2 & --- \\\\\nMin-K\\% Prob \\cite{shi2024_minkprob} & CORAL & 33.2 & 40.4 \\\\\nDetectGPT \\cite{mitchell2023_detectgpt} & best-of-3 & 28.8 & 24.3 \\\\\nRandom & --- & 25.0 & 33.3 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Model Transfer}\n\\label{sec:gen:crossmodel}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-model transfer (leave-one-out, nucleus only). Chance = 25\\% / 33\\% (d1--d3). d1--d3 mean 55.4\\%; QWK\\,=\\,0.81; 94.8\\% errors at $|\\Delta| \\leq 1$.}\n\\label{tab:cross_model}\n\\begin{tabular}{lccccc}\n\\toprule\nHoldout & Acc & d0 & d1 & d2 & d3 \\\\\n & (\\%) & Rec. & Rec. & Rec. & Rec. \\\\\n\\midrule\nPythia & 62.4 & 100.0 & 49.3 & 26.8 & 73.6 \\\\\nOLMo & \\textbf{69.2} & 99.3 & 75.5 & 43.3 & 58.8 \\\\\nGPT-2 XL & 67.6 & 98.5 & 74.7 & 52.6 & 44.5 \\\\\n\\midrule\nMean & 66.4 & 99.3 & 66.5 & 40.9 & 59.0 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nMean transfer accuracy is 66.4\\% (Table~\\ref{tab:cross_model}; d1--d3: 55.4\\%, $2.7{\\times}$ chance); d2 worst case 26.8\\% (Pythia holdout; confusion matrices in Figure~\\ref{fig:confusion_matrix}).\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Strategy Transfer}\n\\label{sec:gen:crossstrategy}\n\nCross-strategy transfer reveals a structural barrier: d1--d3 balanced accuracy drops to 45.9\\% (four-class: 59.1\\%), 7\\,pp below cross-model transfer (Table~\\ref{tab:cross_strategy}).\nA two-stage pipeline (strategy classifier $\\to$ depth classifier) recovers much of this gap (70.5\\%, $+$11.5\\,pp; oracle: 78.3\\%; Appendix~\\ref{app:extended_discussion}).\nOur depth estimation claims hold most strongly within-strategy or when the decoding configuration is known.\n\n%% ----------------------------------------------------------------\n\\subsection{Preliminary Cross-Scale Observations (14B)}\n\\label{sec:gen:crossscale}\n\nAt 14B (Qwen2.5-14B), self-reference yields 50.8\\% ($2{\\times}$ chance, $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}0$); cross-reference control (Pythia-1.4B) drops accuracy to 44.1\\% ($-$6.8\\,pp) but raises $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}$ to 2 for nucleus and temperature, revealing that ${\\sim}$7\\,pp of the accuracy drop is attributable to reference-model mismatch rather than scale (Appendix~\\ref{app:cross_scale_full}).",

Chance = 25\\% / 33.3\\% (d1--d3). $^\\dagger$Adapted from binary detection; Binoculars 11-feat: $p{>}0.05$ vs.\\ 19-feat RF (paired permutation test).}\n\\label{tab:baselines}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcc@{}}\n\\toprule\nMethod & Calibration & 4-Cl (\\%) & d1--d3 (\\%) \\\\\n\\midrule\nRF 19-feature (ours) & RF & 78.4 & \\textbf{71.6} \\\\\nBinoculars$^\\dagger$ \\cite{binoculars2024} & RF (11 feat) & 78.1 & --- \\\\\nMerged (30 feat)$^\\dagger$ & RF & \\textbf{82.6} & --- \\\\\nBinoculars \\cite{binoculars2024} & threshold & 69.8 & --- \\\\\nBinoculars \\cite{binoculars2024} & RF (1 feat) & 63.7 & --- \\\\\nRoBERTa-DCOL (ordinal) & contrastive & 64.5 & --- \\\\\nDeBERTa-v3 (end-to-end) & cross-entropy & 53.1--60.6 & --- \\\\\n\\midrule\nZero-shot LLM (best) & --- & 36.2 & --- \\\\\nMin-K\\% Prob \\cite{shi2024_minkprob} & CORAL & 33.2 & 40.4 \\\\\nDetectGPT \\cite{mitchell2023_detectgpt} & best-of-3 & 28.8 & 24.3 \\\\\nRandom & --- & 25.0 & 33.3 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Model Transfer}\n\\label{sec:gen:crossmodel}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-model transfer (leave-one-out, nucleus only). Chance = 25\\% / 33\\% (d1--d3). d1--d3 mean 55.4\\%; QWK\\,=\\,0.81; 94.8\\% errors at $|\\Delta| \\leq 1$.}\n\\label{tab:cross_model}\n\\begin{tabular}{lccccc}\n\\toprule\nHoldout & Acc & d0 & d1 & d2 & d3 \\\\\n & (\\%) & Rec. & Rec. & Rec. & Rec. \\\\\n\\midrule\nPythia & 62.4 & 100.0 & 49.3 & 26.8 & 73.6 \\\\\nOLMo & \\textbf{69.2} & 99.3 & 75.5 & 43.3 & 58.8 \\\\\nGPT-2 XL & 67.6 & 98.5 & 74.7 & 52.6 & 44.5 \\\\\n\\midrule\nMean & 66.4 & 99.3 & 66.5 & 40.9 & 59.0 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nMean transfer accuracy is 66.4\\% (Table~\\ref{tab:cross_model}; d1--d3: 55.4\\%, $2.7{\\times}$ chance); d2 worst case 26.8\\% (Pythia holdout; confusion matrices in Figure~\\ref{fig:confusion_matrix}).\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Strategy Transfer}\n\\label{sec:gen:crossstrategy}\n\nCross-strategy transfer reveals a structural barrier: d1--d3 balanced accuracy drops to 45.9\\% (four-class: 59.1\\%), 7\\,pp below cross-model transfer (Table~\\ref{tab:cross_strategy}).\nA two-stage pipeline (strategy classifier $\\to$ depth classifier) recovers much of this gap (70.5\\%, $+$11.5\\,pp; oracle: 78.3\\%; Appendix~\\ref{app:extended_discussion}).\nOur depth estimation claims hold most strongly within-strategy or when the decoding configuration is known.\n\n%% ----------------------------------------------------------------\n\\subsection{Preliminary Cross-Scale Observations (14B)}\n\\label{sec:gen:crossscale}\n\nAt 14B (Qwen2.5-14B), self-reference yields 50.8\\% ($2{\\times}$ chance, $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}0$); cross-reference control (Pythia-1.4B) drops accuracy to 44.1\\% ($-$6.8\\,pp) but raises $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}$ to 2 for nucleus and temperature, revealing that ${\\sim}$7\\,pp of the accuracy drop is attributable to reference-model mismatch rather than scale (Appendix~\\ref{app:cross_scale_full}).",

Binoculars convergence with our pipeline is discussed in \\S\\ref{sec:discussion}.",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Multi-channel depth encoding.}\nFour importance measures (Gini, SHAP, permutation, CPI; Kendall's $W{=}0.90$) converge on the same feature ranking, and Binoculars-derived features independently reach 78.1\\% ($p{>}0.05$ vs.\\ 78.4\\%; merged: 82.6\\%).\nDepth signal is a distributional property encoded across multiple statistical channels, not a single exploitable artifact.\n\n\\paragraph{Why neural methods underperform.}\nEnd-to-end classifiers (DeBERTa, RoBERTa-DCOL) lag RF by 14--32\\,pp (\\S\\ref{sec:gen:baselines}) despite full token-level context, confirming that depth is encoded in distributional tail statistics that pretrained representations do not capture; targeted feature engineering is necessary, not merely convenient.\n\n\\paragraph{Feature hierarchy across depths.}\nThe importance transition from \\texttt{var\\_surprisal} at shallow boundaries to perplexity tail statistics at deeper ones (\\S\\ref{sec:atlas:features}) mirrors tail-contraction dynamics: early recursion narrows the surprisal distribution while later steps erode the perplexity tail, consistent with geometric contraction (\\S\\ref{sec:method:theory}).\n\n\\paragraph{Deployment considerations.}\nThe strategy effect (9.3\\,pp) exceeding the model effect (7.2\\,pp) means auditing pipelines must condition on decoding configuration, motivating the two-stage pipeline (\\S\\ref{sec:gen:crossstrategy}) and echoing the call by \\citet{schaeffer2025} for operationally precise metrics.\nScale attenuation at 14B (\\S\\ref{sec:gen:crossscale}), consistent with tail-narrowing theory \\citep{dohmatob2024_tale_of_tails}, may be partially recoverable via scale-matched references.\n\n\\paragraph{Accumulation and outlook.}\nDepth estimation bridges binary detection and model collapse research by providing ordinal resolution \\cite{drayson2025_detection_prevents_collapse}.\nAccumulation experiments at 25--50\\% synthetic mixing (Appendix~\\ref{app:accumulation_auc}) partially validate depth estimation under realistic conditions: $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}3$ is preserved in 5/6 cells, with only GPT-2~XL d2-vs-d3 marginally below threshold (AUC\\,=\\,0.695 vs.\\ $T{=}0.70$).\nExtension to higher mixing ratios (${\\geq}$75\\%), cross-strategy accumulation, prompted generation, and frontier-scale models remains open.\n%% --- 7B SCALE PLACEHOLDER ---\n%% When qwen25_7b_selfref_001 results arrive, add after \"scale-matched references\":\n%% A self-reference probe at 7B (Appendix~\\ref{app:qwen7b_selfref}) yields $X$\\% ($\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}Y$).\n%% --- END PLACEHOLDER ---\n",

_source line 2664 · 2026-05-22T21:44:37.386Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_012Vi6Xr3tmaSeXgzoC9BjHP`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	\paragraph{Multi-channel depth encoding.}
5	Four importance measures (Gini, SHAP, permutation, CPI; Kendall's $W{=}0.90$) converge on the same feature ranking, and Binoculars-derived features independently reach 78.1\% ($p{>}0.05$ vs.\ 78.4\%; merged: 82.6\%).
6	Depth signal is a distributional property encoded across multiple statistical channels, not a single exploitable artifact.
7	
8	\paragraph{Why neural methods underperform.}
9	End-to-end classifiers (DeBERTa, RoBERTa-DCOL) lag RF by 14--32\,pp (\S\ref{sec:gen:baselines}) despite full token-level context, confirming that depth is encoded in distributional tail statistics that pretrained representations do not capture; targeted feature engineering is necessary, not merely convenient.
10	
11	\paragraph{Feature hierarchy across depths.}
12	The importance transition from \texttt{var\_surprisal} at shallow boundaries to perplexity tail statistics at deeper ones (\S\ref{sec:atlas:features}) mirrors tail-contraction dynamics: early recursion narrows the surprisal distribution while later steps erode the perplexity tail, consistent with geometric contraction (\S\ref{sec:method:theory}).
13	
14	\paragraph{Deployment considerations.}
15	The strategy effect (9.3\,pp) exceeding the model effect (7.2\,pp) requires auditing pipelines to condition on decoding configuration (\S\ref{sec:gen:crossstrategy}), echoing \citet{schaeffer2025}; scale attenuation at 14B (\S\ref{sec:gen:crossscale}) may be partially recoverable via scale-matched references.
16	
17	\paragraph{Accumulation and outlook.}
18	Depth estimation bridges binary detection and model collapse research by providing ordinal resolution \cite{drayson2025_detection_prevents_collapse}.
19	Accumulation experiments at 25--50\% synthetic mixing (Appendix~\ref{app:accumulation_auc}) partially validate depth estimation under realistic conditions: $\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}3$ is preserved in 5/6 cells, with only GPT-2~XL d2-vs-d3 marginally below threshold (AUC\,=\,0.695 vs.\ $T{=}0.70$).
20	Extension to higher mixing ratios (${\geq}$75\%), cross-strategy accumulation, prompted generation, and frontier-scale models remains open.
21	%% --- 7B SCALE PLACEHOLDER ---
22	%% When qwen25_7b_selfref_001 results arrive, add after "scale-matched references":
23	%% A self-reference probe at 7B (Appendix~\ref{app:qwen7b_selfref}) yields $X$\% ($\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}Y$).
24	%% --- END PLACEHOLDER ---
25

      "content": "\\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Multi-channel depth encoding.}\nFour importance measures (Gini, SHAP, permutation, CPI; Kendall's $W{=}0.90$) converge on the same feature ranking, and Binoculars-derived features independently reach 78.1\\% ($p{>}0.05$ vs.\\ 78.4\\%; merged: 82.6\\%).\nDepth signal is a distributional property encoded across multiple statistical channels, not a single exploitable artifact.\n\n\\paragraph{Why neural methods underperform.}\nEnd-to-end classifiers (DeBERTa, RoBERTa-DCOL) lag RF by 14--32\\,pp (\\S\\ref{sec:gen:baselines}) despite full token-level context, confirming that depth is encoded in distributional tail statistics that pretrained representations do not capture; targeted feature engineering is necessary, not merely convenient.\n\n\\paragraph{Feature hierarchy across depths.}\nThe importance transition from \\texttt{var\\_surprisal} at shallow boundaries to perplexity tail statistics at deeper ones (\\S\\ref{sec:atlas:features}) mirrors tail-contraction dynamics: early recursion narrows the surprisal distribution while later steps erode the perplexity tail, consistent with geometric contraction (\\S\\ref{sec:method:theory}).\n\n\\paragraph{Deployment considerations.}\nThe strategy effect (9.3\\,pp) exceeding the model effect (7.2\\,pp) requires auditing pipelines to condition on decoding configuration (\\S\\ref{sec:gen:crossstrategy}), echoing \\citet{schaeffer2025};   "content": "[Director 指令] Discussion 改写后 Reviewer 发现 3 个一致性问题，逐条修复。页数极度敏感（恰好 8 页无余量），每条修复都要控制行数。\n\n**修复 1：neural baseline gap 口径不一致**\nDiscussion 写 \"14--32\\,pp\"，§5.1 (generalization.tex:13) 写 \"14--25\\,pp\"。原因是基准不同（per-cell vs 9-cell 平均）。\n→ 统一为 per-cell 口径。§5.1 的 \"14--25\\,pp\" 改为 \"14--32\\,pp\"。如果 §5.1 用的是平均 RF，加个 \"(per-cell)\" 限定词即可。\n\n**修复 2：d2 recall 8.4% 丢失**\n压缩时删掉了 DeBERTa 将 4-class 退化为 d0/d3 二分类（d2 recall 仅 8.4%）的细节。这是 neural method 失败的关键机制证据。\n→ 在 Discussion 的 \"Why neural methods underperform\" 段末尾加半句，如 \"(with d2 recall as low as 8\\%)\" 即可，不需要完整句子。\n\n**修复 3：arXiv 74.1% 主文丢失**\nIntroduction contribution 3 仍引用 \"cross-domain arXiv 74.1%\"，但 §5.4 压缩时删掉了对应段落。审稿人从 Intro 跳到 §5 会找不到。\n→ 在 §5.4 末尾加一句 \"Cross-domain transfer to arXiv abstracts reaches 74.1\\% (Appendix~\\ref{app:cross_domain}).\"\n\n三条改完后编译确认 ≤ 8 页。禁止 em dash。"

_source line 2687 · 2026-05-22T22:21:28.533Z_

[Director 指令] Discussion 改写后 Reviewer 发现 3 个一致性问题，逐条修复。页数极度敏感（恰好 8 页无余量），每条修复都要控制行数。

**修复 1：neural baseline gap 口径不一致**
Discussion 写 "14--32\,pp"，§5.1 (generalization.tex:13) 写 "14--25\,pp"。原因是基准不同（per-cell vs 9-cell 平均）。
→ 统一为 per-cell 口径。§5.1 的 "14--25\,pp" 改为 "14--32\,pp"。如果 §5.1 用的是平均 RF，加个 "(per-cell)" 限定词即可。

**修复 2：d2 recall 8.4% 丢失**
压缩时删掉了 DeBERTa 将 4-class 退化为 d0/d3 二分类（d2 recall 仅 8.4%）的细节。这是 neural method 失败的关键机制证据。
→ 在 Discussion 的 "Why neural methods underperform" 段末尾加半句，如 "(with d2 recall as low as 8\%)" 即可，不需要完整句子。

**修复 3：arXiv 74.1% 主文丢失**
Introduction contribution 3 仍引用 "cross-domain arXiv 74.1%"，但 §5.4 压缩时删掉了对应段落。审稿人从 Intro 跳到 §5 会找不到。
→ 在 §5.4 末尾加一句 "Cross-domain transfer to arXiv abstracts reaches 74.1\% (Appendix~\ref{app:cross_domain})."

三条改完后编译确认 ≤ 8 页。禁止 em dash。

    "originalFile": "\\section{Generalization and Robustness}\n\\label{sec:generalization}\n\nWe test whether atlas accuracy (68.9--85.9\\% four-class; d1--d3: 58.9--81.5\\%) reflects genuine statistical regularities across eight generalization axes (five below, three in Appendix).\n\n\\subsection{Classifier Comparison and Surface-Text Ablation}\n\\label{sec:gen:baselines}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:baselines} compares methods across all 9 cells.\nFour classifiers (RF, XGBoost, ordinal MLP, prototypical) converge within 0.8\\,pp under cross-model transfer (Appendix~\\ref{app:learned_estimator}), confirming a feature-determined accuracy ceiling.\n\n\\paragraph{Neural end-to-end baselines.}\nDeBERTa-v3-base (LoRA, 53--61\\%) and RoBERTa-DCOL (ordinal contrastive, 64.5\\%) lag RF by 14--25\\,pp; implications are discussed in \\S\\ref{sec:discussion}.\n\n\\paragraph{Binary detectors and membership inference.}\nPerturbation-based (DetectGPT, 28.8\\%) and membership-inference (Min-K\\% Prob, 33.2\\%) methods produce scores with no ordinal resolution (Table~\\ref{tab:baselines}); Binoculars d1v2 AUC\\,=\\,0.50--0.53 vs.\\ RF 0.93 (Appendix~\\ref{app:detector_baselines}). Binoculars convergence with our pipeline is discussed in \\S\\ref{sec:discussion}.\n\n\\paragraph{Surface-text ablation.}\nSingle-feature regression on mean perplexity (25.1\\%) and zero-shot LLMs (best 36.2\\%; Appendix~\\ref{app:zeroshot_prompt}) confirm depth signal is inaccessible from surface text.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Method comparison (RF mean across 9 cells, 5-fold CV). Chance = 25\\% / 33.3\\% (d1--d3). $^\\dagger$Adapted from binary detection; Binoculars 11-feat: $p{>}0.05$ vs.\\ 19-feat RF (paired permutation test).}\n\\label{tab:baselines}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcc@{}}\n\\toprule\nMethod & Calibration & 4-Cl (\\%) & d1--d3 (\\%) \\\\\n\\midrule\nRF 19-feature (ours) & RF & 78.4 & \\textbf{71.6} \\\\\nBinoculars$^\\dagger$ \\cite{binoculars2024} & RF (11 feat) & 78.1 & --- \\\\\nMerged (30 feat)$^\\dagger$ & RF & \\textbf{82.6} & --- \\\\\nBinoculars \\cite{binoculars2024} & threshold & 69.8 & --- \\\\\nBinoculars \\cite{binoculars2024} & RF (1 feat) & 63.7 & --- \\\\\nRoBERTa-DCOL (ordinal) & contrastive & 64.5 & --- \\\\\nDeBERTa-v3 (end-to-end) & cross-entropy & 53.1--60.6 & --- \\\\\n\\midrule\nZero-shot LLM (best) & --- & 36.2 & --- \\\\\nMin-K\\% Prob \\cite{shi2024_minkprob} & CORAL & 33.2 & 40.4 \\\\\nDetectGPT \\cite{mitchell2023_detectgpt} & best-of-3 & 28.8 & 24.3 \\\\\nRandom & --- & 25.0 & 33.3 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Model Transfer}\n\\label{sec:gen:crossmodel}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-model transfer (leave-one-out, nucleus only). Chance = 25\\% / 33\\% (d1--d3). d1--d3 mean 55.4\\%; QWK\\,=\\,0.81; 94.8\\% errors at $|\\Delta| \\leq 1$.}\n\\label{tab:cross_model}\n\\begin{tabular}{lccccc}\n\\toprule\nHoldout & Acc & d0 & d1 & d2 & d3 \\\\\n & (\\%) & Rec. & Rec. & Rec. & Rec. \\\\\n\\midrule\nPythia & 62.4 & 100.0 & 49.3 & 26.8 & 73.6 \\\\\nOLMo & \\textbf{69.2} & 99.3 & 75.5 & 43.3 & 58.8 \\\\\nGPT-2 XL & 67.6 & 98.5 & 74.7 & 52.6 & 44.5 \\\\\n\\midrule\nMean & 66.4 & 99.3 & 66.5 & 40.9 & 59.0 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nMean transfer accuracy is 66.4\\% (Table~\\ref{tab:cross_model}; d1--d3: 55.4\\%, $2.7{\\times}$ chance); d2 worst case 26.8\\% (Pythia holdout; confusion matrices in Figure~\\ref{fig:confusion_matrix}).\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Strategy Transfer}\n\\label{sec:gen:crossstrategy}\n\nCross-strategy transfer reveals a structural barrier: d1--d3 balanced accuracy drops to 45.9\\% (four-class: 59.1\\%), 7\\,pp below cross-model transfer (Table~\\ref{tab:cross_strategy}).\nA two-stage pipeline (strategy classifier $\\to$ depth classifier) recovers much of this gap (70.5\\%, $+$11.5\\,pp; oracle: 78.3\\%; Appendix~\\ref{app:extended_discussion}).\nOur depth estimation claims hold most strongly within-strategy or when the decoding configuration is known.\n\n%% ----------------------------------------------------------------\n\\subsection{Preliminary Cross-Scale Observations (14B)}\n\\label{sec:gen:crossscale}\n\nAt 14B (Qwen2.5-14B), self-reference yields 50.8\\% ($2{\\times}$ chance, $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}0$); cross-reference control (Pythia-1.4B) drops accuracy to 44.1\\% ($-$6.8\\,pp) but raises $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}$ to 2 for nucleus and temperature, revealing that ${\\sim}$7\\,pp of the accuracy drop is attributable to reference-model mismatch rather than scale (Appendix~\\ref{app:cross_scale_full}).",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Multi-channel depth encoding.}\nFour importance measures (Gini, SHAP, permutation, CPI; Kendall's $W{=}0.90$) converge on the same feature ranking, and Binoculars-derived features independently reach 78.1\\% ($p{>}0.05$ vs.\\ 78.4\\%; merged: 82.6\\%).\nDepth signal is a distributional property encoded across multiple statistical channels, not a single exploitable artifact.\n\n\\paragraph{Why neural methods underperform.}\nEnd-to-end classifiers (DeBERTa, RoBERTa-DCOL) lag RF by 14--32\\,pp (\\S\\ref{sec:gen:baselines}) despite full token-level context, confirming that depth is encoded in distributional tail statistics that pretrained representations do not capture; targeted feature engineering is necessary, not merely convenient.\n\n\\paragraph{Feature hierarchy across depths.}\nThe importance transition from \\texttt{var\\_surprisal} at shallow boundaries to perplexity tail statistics at deeper ones (\\S\\ref{sec:atlas:features}) mirrors tail-contraction dynamics: early recursion narrows the surprisal distribution while later steps erode the perplexity tail, consistent with geometric contraction (\\S\\ref{sec:method:theory}).\n\n\\paragraph{Deployment considerations.}\nThe strategy effect (9.3\\,pp) exceeding the model effect (7.2\\,pp) requires auditing pipelines to condition on decoding configuration (\\S\\ref{sec:gen:crossstrategy}), echoing \\citet{schaeffer2025}; scale attenuation at 14B (\\S\\ref{sec:gen:crossscale}) may be partially recoverable via scale-matched references.\n\n\\paragraph{Accumulation and outlook.}\nDepth estimation bridges binary detection and model collapse research by providing ordinal resolution \\cite{drayson2025_detection_prevents_collapse}.\nAccumulation experiments at 25--50\\% synthetic mixing (Appendix~\\ref{app:accumulation_auc}) partially validate depth estimation under realistic conditions: $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}3$ is preserved in 5/6 cells, with only GPT-2~XL d2-vs-d3 marginally below threshold (AUC\\,=\\,0.695 vs.\\ $T{=}0.70$).\nExtension to higher mixing ratios (${\\geq}$75\\%), cross-strategy accumulation, prompted generation, and frontier-scale models remains open.\n%% --- 7B SCALE PLACEHOLDER ---\n%% When qwen25_7b_selfref_001 results arrive, add after \"scale-matched references\":\n%% A self-reference probe at 7B (Appendix~\\ref{app:qwen7b_selfref}) yields $X$\\% ($\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}Y$).\n%% --- END PLACEHOLDER ---\n",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Generalization and Robustness}\n\\label{sec:generalization}\n\nWe test whether atlas accuracy (68.9--85.9\\% four-class; d1--d3: 58.9--81.5\\%) reflects genuine statistical regularities across eight generalization axes (five below, three in Appendix).\n\n\\subsection{Classifier Comparison and Surface-Text Ablation}\n\\label{sec:gen:baselines}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:baselines} compares methods across all 9 cells.\nFour classifiers (RF, XGBoost, ordinal MLP, prototypical) converge within 0.8\\,pp under cross-model transfer (Appendix~\\ref{app:learned_estimator}), confirming a feature-determined accuracy ceiling.\n\n\\paragraph{Neural end-to-end baselines.}\nDeBERTa-v3-base (LoRA, 53--61\\%) and RoBERTa-DCOL (ordinal contrastive, 64.5\\%) lag RF by 14--32\\,pp per cell; implications are discussed in \\S\\ref{sec:discussion}.\n\n\\paragraph{Binary detectors and membership inference.}\nPerturbation-based (DetectGPT, 28.8\\%) and membership-inference (Min-K\\% Prob, 33.2\\%) methods produce scores with no ordinal resolution (Table~\\ref{tab:baselines}); Binoculars d1v2 AUC\\,=\\,0.50--0.53 vs.\\ RF 0.93 (Appendix~\\ref{app:detector_baselines}). Binoculars convergence with our pipeline is discussed in \\S\\ref{sec:discussion}.\n\n\\paragraph{Surface-text ablation.}\nSingle-feature regression on mean perplexity (25.1\\%) and zero-shot LLMs (best 36.2\\%; Appendix~\\ref{app:zeroshot_prompt}) confirm depth signal is inaccessible from surface text.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Method comparison (RF mean across 9 cells, 5-fold CV). Chance = 25\\% / 33.3\\% (d1--d3). $^\\dagger$Adapted from binary detection; Binoculars 11-feat: $p{>}0.05$ vs.\\ 19-feat RF (paired permutation test).}\n\\label{tab:baselines}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcc@{}}\n\\toprule\nMethod & Calibration & 4-Cl (\\%) & d1--d3 (\\%) \\\\\n\\midrule\nRF 19-feature (ours) & RF & 78.4 & \\textbf{71.6} \\\\\nBinoculars$^\\dagger$ \\cite{binoculars2024} & RF (11 feat) & 78.1 & --- \\\\\nMerged (30 feat)$^\\dagger$ & RF & \\textbf{82.6} & --- \\\\\nBinoculars \\cite{binoculars2024} & threshold & 69.8 & --- \\\\\nBinoculars \\cite{binoculars2024} & RF (1 feat) & 63.7 & --- \\\\\nRoBERTa-DCOL (ordinal) & contrastive & 64.5 & --- \\\\\nDeBERTa-v3 (end-to-end) & cross-entropy & 53.1--60.6 & --- \\\\\n\\midrule\nZero-shot LLM (best) & --- & 36.2 & --- \\\\\nMin-K\\% Prob \\cite{shi2024_minkprob} & CORAL & 33.2 & 40.4 \\\\\nDetectGPT \\cite{mitchell2023_detectgpt} & best-of-3 & 28.8 & 24.3 \\\\\nRandom & --- & 25.0 & 33.3 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Model Transfer}\n\\label{sec:gen:crossmodel}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-model transfer (leave-one-out, nucleus only). Chance = 25\\% / 33\\% (d1--d3). d1--d3 mean 55.4\\%; QWK\\,=\\,0.81; 94.8\\% errors at $|\\Delta| \\leq 1$.}\n\\label{tab:cross_model}\n\\begin{tabular}{lccccc}\n\\toprule\nHoldout & Acc & d0 & d1 & d2 & d3 \\\\\n & (\\%) & Rec. & Rec. & Rec. & Rec. \\\\\n\\midrule\nPythia & 62.4 & 100.0 & 49.3 & 26.8 & 73.6 \\\\\nOLMo & \\textbf{69.2} & 99.3 & 75.5 & 43.3 & 58.8 \\\\\nGPT-2 XL & 67.6 & 98.5 & 74.7 & 52.6 & 44.5 \\\\\n\\midrule\nMean & 66.4 & 99.3 & 66.5 & 40.9 & 59.0 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nMean transfer accuracy is 66.4\\% (Table~\\ref{tab:cross_model}; d1--d3: 55.4\\%, $2.7{\\times}$ chance); d2 worst case 26.8\\% (Pythia holdout; confusion matrices in Figure~\\ref{fig:confusion_matrix}).\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Strategy Transfer}\n\\label{sec:gen:crossstrategy}\n\nCross-strategy transfer reveals a structural barrier: d1--d3 balanced accuracy drops to 45.9\\% (four-class: 59.1\\%), 7\\,pp below cross-model transfer (Table~\\ref{tab:cross_strategy}).\nA two-stage pipeline (strategy classifier $\\to$ depth classifier) recovers much of this gap (70.5\\%, $+$11.5\\,pp; oracle: 78.3\\%; Appendix~\\ref{app:extended_discussion}).\nOur depth estimation claims hold most strongly within-strategy or when the decoding configuration is known.\n\n%% ----------------------------------------------------------------\n\\subsection{Preliminary Cross-Scale Observations (14B)}\n\\label{sec:gen:crossscale}\n\nAt 14B (Qwen2.5-14B), self-reference yields 50.8\\% ($2{\\times}$ chance, $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}0$); cross-reference control (Pythia-1.4B) drops accuracy to 44.1\\% ($-$6.8\\,pp) but raises $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}$ to 2 for nucleus and temperature, revealing that ${\\sim}$7\\,pp of the accuracy drop is attributable to reference-model mismatch rather than scale (Appendix~\\ref{app:cross_scale_full}).",

[Director 指令] Contribution 3 重构 + Abstract 微调。页数恰好 8 页无余量，所有改动必须 net-zero 行数或更少。

**任务 1：拆分 Contribution 3**

当前 introduction.tex:33 的 Contribution 3 把 5 个维度塞一条 bullet（cross-model, cross-domain, 14B, accumulation, cross-strategy）。拆分为两条更聚焦的 contribution：

```latex
    \item \textbf{Accumulation robustness.} Under 25--50\% human-text mixing, $\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}3$ is preserved in 5/6 model--strategy cells despite $-$8--15\,pp accuracy attenuation, validating ecological relevance beyond full-replacement chains.

    \item \textbf{Generalization boundaries.} Cross-model transfer reaches 66.4\%, cross-domain (arXiv) 74.1\%, and 14B cross-reference $\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}2$; cross-strategy transfer (d1--d3: 45.9\%) and a two-stage routing pipeline (+11.5\,pp) delineate the method's deployment envelope.
```

注意：两条加起来的行数必须 ≤ 原来那条的行数。如果超了，压缩措辞。

**任务 2：Abstract 微调**

当前 Abstract 的 three findings 和 Contribution 列表有两个错位：
- Finding 2 "strategy > model" 在 contribution 列表无对应
- Two-stage pipeline 是唯一 practical method contribution 但 Abstract 没提

在 abstract.tex 做最小改动：
- Abstract line 7（"Perturbation-based..."那句之前）加半句提 two-stage pipeline，如 "A two-stage strategy-routing pipeline partially recovers cross-strategy performance (+11.5\,pp)."
- 但要注意 Abstract 总长度不要膨胀太多

**约束**：
- 编译确认 ≤ 8 页
- 禁止 em dash

27	Our contributions are:
28	\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=*]
29	    \item \textbf{Task formalization and metric.} We define recursive generation depth estimation as ordinal classification, introduce the \emph{Effective Discriminable Depth} (EDD) metric, and construct a 180K cell-instance benchmark ($3$ model families $\times$ $3$ decoding strategies $\times$ $4$ depths; 140K unique passages).
30	
31	    \item \textbf{Depth Degradation Atlas.} The perplexity tail feature family and surprisal variance jointly dominate depth discrimination across all 9 cells; $\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}3$ in 8/9 cells (4 under nucleus depth extension), validated by 5-class extension (71--80\%); feature group ablation confirms perplexity tail dominance (76.6\% alone vs.\ 78.4\% full set).
32	
33	    \item \textbf{Systematic characterization.} Cross-model transfer 66.4\% (d1--d3: 55.4\%), cross-domain arXiv 74.1\%, 14B cross-reference 44.1\% ($\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}2$); 25--50\% accumulation preserves $\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}3$ in 5/6 cells despite $-$8--15\,pp accuracy attenuation. We identify cross-strategy transfer (d1--d3: 45.9\%) as a boundary condition.
34	\end{itemize}

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_012P4wznY2Gbxgj4peedhWSK`</summary>

```text
1	\begin{abstract}
2	As language models increasingly train on web text containing their own outputs, a recursive model$\to$data$\to$model loop arises, yet no method estimates how many such iterations a text has undergone.
3	We formalize \emph{recursive generation depth estimation} as ordinal classification and construct a benchmark of 180K cell-instances (140K unique passages) spanning three model families, three decoding strategies, and four depths under controlled full-replacement chains at the 1--1.5B parameter scale.
4	A lightweight pipeline (19 statistical features classified by Random Forest) achieves d1--d3 balanced accuracy of 59--82\% across nine model--strategy cells, with $\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70} = 3$ in 8/9 cells.
5	Three findings emerge: (1)~depth signal concentrates in perplexity distribution tails, corroborated by classifier convergence and independent cross-perplexity features; (2)~decoding strategy affects cross-condition transfer more than model architecture, suggesting structurally distinct degradation trajectories; (3)~signal attenuates with scale (14B cross-reference accuracy ${\sim}44$\%, $\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}2$ for nucleus and temperature, 0 for top-$k$), with reference-model choice accounting for ${\sim}7$\,pp of the apparent degradation.
6	Our depth estimation claims hold most strongly within-strategy or when the decoding configuration is known.
7	Perturbation-based and membership-inference baselines lack ordinal depth resolution, confirming that depth estimation requires distributional statistics beyond binary detection scores.
8	\end{abstract}
9	
```

    "originalFile": "\\begin{abstract}\nAs language models increasingly train on web text containing their own outputs, a recursive model$\\to$data$\\to$model loop arises, yet no method estimates how many such iterations a text has undergone.\nWe formalize \\emph{recursive generation depth estimation} as ordinal classification and construct a benchmark of 180K cell-instances (140K unique passages) spanning three model families, three decoding strategies, and four depths under controlled full-replacement chains at the 1--1.5B parameter scale.\nA lightweight pipeline (19 statistical features classified by Random Forest) achieves d1--d3 balanced accuracy of 59--82\\% across nine model--strategy cells, with $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70} = 3$ in 8/9 cells.\nThree findings emerge: (1)~depth signal concentrates in perplexity distribution tails, corroborated by classifier convergence and independent cross-perplexity features; (2)~decoding strategy affects cross-condition transfer more than model architecture, suggesting structurally distinct degradation trajectories; (3)~signal attenuates with scale (14B cross-reference accuracy ${\\sim}44$\\%, $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}2$ for nucleus and temperature, 0 for top-$k$), with reference-model choice accounting for ${\\sim}7$\\,pp of the apparent degradation.\nOur depth estimation claims hold most strongly within-strategy or when the decoding configuration is known.\nPerturbation-based and membership-inference baselines lack ordinal depth resolution, confirming that depth estimation requires distributional statistics beyond binary detection scores.\n\\end{abstract}\n",

          " A lightweight pipeline (19 statistical features classified by Random Forest) achieves d1--d3 balanced accuracy of 59--82\\% across nine model--strategy cells, with $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70} = 3$ in 8/9 cells.",

    "oldString": "    \\item \\textbf{Systematic characterization.} Cross-model transfer 66.4\\% (d1--d3: 55.4\\%), cross-domain arXiv 74.1\\%, 14B cross-reference 44.1\\% ($\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}2$); 25--50\\% accumulation preserves $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}3$ in 5/6 cells despite $-$8--15\\,pp accuracy attenuation. We identify cross-strategy transfer (d1--d3: 45.9\\%) as a boundary condition.",
    "newString": "    \\item \\textbf{Accumulation robustness.} Under 25--50\\% synthetic mixing, $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}3$ is preserved in 5/6 cells despite $-$8--15\\,pp accuracy attenuation.\n\n    \\item \\textbf{Generalization boundaries.} Cross-model 66.4\\%, cross-domain (arXiv) 74.1\\%, 14B cross-reference $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}2$; cross-strategy (d1--d3: 45.9\\%) is a boundary condition, partially recovered by two-stage routing ($+$11.5\\,pp).",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\n\\label{sec:intro}\n\nThe text on which large language models are trained increasingly contains their own outputs.\nAs synthetic content proliferates across the web, a recursive loop emerges: models trained on web corpora ingest text generated by earlier models, and their outputs feed into the next generation's training data \\cite{shumailov2024_nature,alemohammad2024_mad}.\nThis model$\\to$data$\\to$model cycle is no longer hypothetical; it is already occurring in the wild. Yet whether the recursive generation depth of a text can be estimated from its statistical properties remains unstudied, a question that matters because first-generation synthetic text may be benign or even beneficial for training, while deeply recursive text drives the distributional collapse that degrades model quality \\cite{dohmatob2024_tale_of_tails}.\nWe focus on the 1--1.5B parameter regime under \\emph{controlled full-replacement chains}, where each generation fully replaces the prior training set, isolating the depth variable. Real-world contamination involves partial mixing at unknown proportions; full replacement provides an upper bound on depth signal strength (\\S\\ref{sec:discussion}).\n\nExisting tools address adjacent but distinct problems.\nBinary detectors \\cite{binoculars2024,fast_detectgpt2024,diveye2026} collapse all depths $d \\geq 1$ into a single category, a limitation we confirm empirically: their pairwise d1-vs-d2 AUC collapses to chance level (\\S\\ref{sec:gen:baselines}); watermarking \\cite{kirchenbauer2023_watermark} requires proactive embedding.\nThe model collapse literature \\cite{shumailov2024_nature,dohmatob2024_tale_of_tails} assumes depth is known a priori; \\citet{drayson2025_detection_prevents_collapse} filter synthetic data via binary detection without distinguishing depths.\nNo existing method estimates the ordinal recursive generation depth of a text passage from its content alone (Figure~\\ref{fig:problem}).\n\nWe find that recursive generation leaves quantifiable statistical fingerprints in text, and these fingerprints are \\textbf{consistent in direction but scale-dependent in magnitude} across the three model families and three decoding strategies studied.\nSpecifically, the perplexity tail feature family (\\texttt{p90\\_ppl}, \\texttt{p75\\_ppl}, \\texttt{kurtosis\\_ppl}) and surprisal variance emerge as dominant depth indicators, with group-level dominance robust across all 9 model--strategy configurations and three importance measures.\nOur contribution centers on task formalization and systematic empirical characterization; the convergence of four classifiers within 0.8\\,pp (\\S\\ref{sec:gen:baselines}) confirms that signal resides in feature design, not classifier architecture.\nWe formalize recursive generation depth estimation as ordinal classification and find that a lightweight pipeline suffices: compute 19 reference-model statistical features (effective dimensionality ${\\approx}$4--5 due to multicollinearity; \\S\\ref{sec:atlas:features}) and classify with a Random Forest into $K{+}1$ depth classes.\nTo characterize how recursive generation reshapes these features, we construct the \\emph{Depth Degradation Atlas}: a $3 \\times 3$ grid of model families $\\times$ decoding strategies spanning 4 depths (180K cell-instances).\nThe atlas reveals strategy-dependent degradation trajectories; we introduce \\emph{Effective Discriminable Depth} (EDD) to quantify how far into the recursion chain adjacent depths remain distinguishable.\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.68\\textwidth]{figures/fig_problem_illustration.pdf}\n\\caption{Recursive generation depth estimation. A seed corpus $\\mathcal{D}_0$ feeds a LoRA finetuning--sampling loop producing successively deeper corpora. Given a passage~$x$, the task is to estimate its ordinal depth~$d$.}\n\\label{fig:problem}\n\\end{figure*}\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=*]\n    \\item \\textbf{Task formalization and metric.} We define recursive generation depth estimation as ordinal classification, introduce the \\emph{Effective Discriminable Depth} (EDD) metric, and construct a 180K cell-instance benchmark ($3$ model families $\\times$ $3$ decoding strategies $\\times$ $4$ depths; 140K unique passages).\n\n    \\item \\textbf{Depth Degradation Atlas.} The perplexity tail feature family and surprisal variance jointly dominate depth discrimination across all 9 cells; $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}3$ in 8/9 cells (4 under nucleus depth extension), validated by 5-class extension (71--80\\%); feature group ablation confirms perplexity tail dominance (76.6\\% alone vs.\\ 78.4\\% full set).\n\n    \\item \\textbf{Systematic characterization.} Cross-model transfer 66.4\\% (d1--d3: 55.4\\%), cross-domain arXiv 74.1\\%, 14B cross-reference 44.1\\% ($\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}2$); 25--50\\% accumulation preserves $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}3$ in 5/6 cells despite $-$8--15\\,pp accuracy attenuation. We identify cross-strategy transfer (d1--d3: 45.9\\%) as a boundary condition.\n\\end{itemize}",

```text
1	\section*{Limitations}
2	\label{sec:limitations}
3	
4	\paragraph{Full-replacement vs.\ accumulation.}
5	The primary benchmark uses full-replacement chains; real-world contamination involves accumulation at unknown mixing ratios \cite{gerstgrasser2024_accumulate}.
6	Accumulation experiments (Appendix~\ref{app:accumulation_auc}) show accuracy attenuation that increases with higher synthetic mixing ratios, and do not test cross-strategy accumulation; extension to larger models and higher mixing ratios remains future work.
7	
8	\paragraph{Scale, generalization, and reference model.}
9	Depth signal attenuates at larger scales (\S\ref{sec:gen:crossscale}), with reference-model mismatch contributing to the observed degradation.
10	Cross-strategy transfer can invert adjacent-depth predictions when the classifier's training strategy differs from the test data, requiring strategy-aware deployment.
11	The pipeline requires a fixed reference model; cross-architecture references degrade accuracy (Appendix~\ref{app:ref_ablation}).
12	Additional caveats (single LoRA seed, web-crawl false positive rates, passage length sensitivity) are detailed in the Appendix.
13	
14	\section*{Ethics Statement}
15	
16	This work uses publicly available pretrained language models (Pythia-1.4B, OLMo-1B, GPT-2~XL) and Wikipedia text as seed data.
17	No human subjects were involved, no private or sensitive data were collected, and no new models were released.
18	Our method is intended to support data provenance and training-data auditing; we acknowledge that depth estimation tools could in principle be misused to circumvent content moderation, though such risks are shared with all detection-oriented research.
19	Code and data will be released upon acceptance.
20	AI assistants were used in an auxiliary role for coding support and language polishing.
21	All scientific decisions, experimental design, analysis, and core writing were conducted by the authors.
```

</details>
